### Saved output: 4484324f-e3b2-4dcf-95c1-ffc86db299f5

#### Explanation

The failed run used an over-escaped filename regex that required a backslash before the log extension, so none of the normal YYYY-MM-DD_source.log filenames matched \[T0B2:&lt;RANGE&gt;pattern = re.compile(r'^(\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2})_.*\\.log$')&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. It then accepted an all-zero CSV as valid and incorrectly concluded that this was consistent with the available data \[T0B5:&lt;RANGE&gt;all of which are zero indicating no matching log entries for the specified severities in any period.&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. By contrast, the successful run inspected an actual file containing WARNING, INFO, and ERROR records \[T1B4:&lt;RANGE&gt;2025-08-12 14:19:56 \[WARNING\] Disk space low: 2740 remaining
2025-08-12 10:47:49 \[INFO\] Scheduled backup completed successfully
2025-08-12 08:56:53 \[INFO\] Database connection established
2025-08-12 06:31:19 \[INFO\] Scheduled backup completed successfully
2025-08-12 04:26:16 \[ERROR\] Deadlock detected in transaction ID 4839&lt;/RANGE&gt;\], recognized the regex error after seeing zeros \[T1B7:&lt;RANGE&gt;All counts in summary.csv are zero because the regex pattern used when scanning log filenames mistakenly required a literal backslash before .log, so no files matched.&lt;/RANGE&gt;\], and reran with the corrected pattern. That correction produced substantial nonzero counts, confirming that the failed run had skipped every input file \[T1B10:&lt;RANGE&gt;today,ERROR,370
today,WARNING,463
today,INFO,1433&lt;/RANGE&gt;\].

#### Cause Summary

An over-escaped filename regex caused every log file to be skipped.

### Saved output: e20c01e4-f26b-4354-8984-f907cb5d7091

#### Explanation

The failed run omitted `KrJiw0OZx7jf.jpg` from its invoice candidates and moved it to `/app/other`, despite that file being present in the original document list \[T0B2:&lt;RANGE&gt;KrJiw0OZx7jf.jpg&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. Its final verification visibly showed the file under `other` and absent from `invoices`, but the agent still declared completion \[T0B94:&lt;RANGE&gt;6NVuAIhTV4KB.jpg  F0oZMhSUm2dO.jpg  GFAlpKoFg81H.pdf  KrJiw0OZx7jf.jpg  QOoA_j33PD_E.jpg  WqWMArQQlSMv.jpg  dvkRkFVFhHga.pdf  dx0AWchV01ZJ.pdf&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. By contrast, the successful run OCRed this document, identified it as an invoice with `Total: $9963` rather than the different amount due \[T1B50:&lt;RANGE&gt;Invoice number 257667
Date of issue Oct. 19, 2023
Date due Nov. 21, 2023&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] \[T1B50:&lt;RANGE&gt;SubTotal: $9963
Total: $9963

Amount due: $7139 USD&lt;/RANGE&gt;\], and included it in both the invoice directory and CSV \[T1B58:&lt;RANGE&gt;KrJiw0OZx7jf.jpg,9963.00,0.00&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. This missing invoice made the failed CSV aggregate `71352.20` instead of the successful total `81315.20` \[T0B94:&lt;RANGE&gt;total,71352.20,5402.48&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] \[T1B58:&lt;RANGE&gt;total,81315.20,5402.48&lt;/RANGE&gt;\].

#### Cause Summary

One invoice was misclassified, omitted from the CSV, and moved to other.

### Saved output: 7ee45546-e813-44ff-992c-fff09261d385

#### Explanation

The failed run spent much of its wall-clock budget installing R and repeatedly polling the lengthy installation \[T0B3:&lt;RANGE&gt;apt-get install -y r-base\n&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] \[T0B5:&lt;RANGE&gt;Wait for the apt-get install command to finish.&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. Its decisive failure was repeatedly sending oversized file-creation commands that silently failed, leaving `/app/ars.R` absent when it attempted to source and test it \[T0B24:&lt;RANGE&gt;cannot open file '/app/ars.R': No such file or directory&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] \[T0B30:&lt;RANGE&gt;head: cannot open '/app/ars.R' for reading: No such file or directory&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. The final workaround created only a deliberately truncated 7,552-byte file containing “# ... (truncated in this script for brevity),” so the required complete `ars` and `test` implementation was never delivered or verified before timeout \[T0B31:&lt;RANGE&gt;# ... (truncated in this script for brevity)&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] \[T0B32:&lt;RANGE&gt;-rw-r--r-- 1 root root 7552 Dec 11 18:19 ars.R&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. By contrast, the successful run produced a 271-line implementation, explicitly inspected it, and executed the test function far enough to confirm valid normal samples with the required statistics \[T1B82:&lt;RANGE&gt;271 /app/ars.R&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] \[T1B84:&lt;RANGE&gt;NORMAL_TEST: PASS (mean=0.007, sd=0.994)&lt;/RANGE&gt;\].

#### Cause Summary

Oversized write commands failed, leaving only a truncated, unverified R implementation.

### Saved output: ec42bd06-9edc-4b74-af6d-deb02e970a6d

#### Explanation

The failed run spent most of its time repeatedly trying variations of unreadable ASCII rendering rather than switching promptly to OCR \[T0B11:&lt;RANGE&gt;Re-run the ASCII conversion at higher width and with a simpler two-character palette for better contrast.&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] \[T0B39:&lt;RANGE&gt;Adjust the ASCII rendering by applying an aspect ratio correction&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. It eventually installed Tesseract, but the lengthy package installation consumed the remaining wall-clock budget, and the run ended immediately after installation completed \[T0B41:&lt;RANGE&gt;Install tesseract-ocr.&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] \[T0B52:&lt;RANGE&gt;Setting up tesseract-ocr (5.3.0-2) ...&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] \[R0M.exception\]. Consequently, it never ran OCR, derived the hashing logic, or created `/app/output.txt`; by contrast, the successful run extracted the pseudocode with Tesseract \[T1B56:&lt;RANGE&gt;def main():
GALT = 6"0000TBENCH-SALT”&lt;/RANGE&gt;\], computed the two SHA-256 operations, wrote the required file, and verified its contents \[T1B57:&lt;RANGE&gt;with open('output.txt', 'w') as f:
    f.write(h + '\n')&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] \[T1B58:&lt;RANGE&gt;bee26a133f103b9ecda444c70ec22cafef6e31a3de7af6d047974dc90ce3defe&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. The decisive failure was therefore poor time allocation: too many ineffective image-rendering experiments left insufficient time to execute and verify the actual solution.

#### Cause Summary

Repeated ASCII experiments and a late OCR install caused timeout before producing output.

```json
  }
```

### Saved output: 2ea8829c-fcf7-48da-b457-bae55450a59f

#### Explanation

The failed run misidentified the vulnerability as broad exception suppression in `MultiDict.get` and reported CWE-703 \[T0B51:&lt;RANGE&gt;printf '{"file_path": "/app/bottle.py", "cwe_id": \["cwe-703"\]}\n' &gt; report.jsonl&lt;/RANGE&gt;\], even though the decisive failing test required rejecting control characters in HTTP headers with `ValueError` \[T0B62:&lt;RANGE&gt;self.assertRaises(ValueError, func, value, "test-value")
            self.assertRaises(ValueError, func, "test-name", value)&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. It later added the correct header validation and achieved 367 passing tests \[T0B80:&lt;RANGE&gt;367 passed in 0.58s&lt;/RANGE&gt;\], but never corrected `report.jsonl`, leaving the exact CWE requirement unsatisfied. By contrast, the successful run used the baseline test failure to identify CRLF/header injection as CWE-93 \[T1B21:&lt;RANGE&gt;document the vulnerability in /app/report.jsonl with CWE ID 93.&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] and wrote the expected report entry \[T1B22:&lt;RANGE&gt;{"file_path": "/app/bottle.py", "cwe_id": \["cwe-93"\]}&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. Thus, the failure was caused by incorrect vulnerability classification and report contents, compounded by an unnecessary unrelated modification to `MultiDict.get` \[T0B54:&lt;RANGE&gt;except (KeyError, IndexError):
            return default&lt;/RANGE&gt;\].

#### Cause Summary

Reported CWE-703 instead of the required CWE-93 header-injection vulnerability.

## Result group 6

### Inputs

```json
{

  },
  "successful_run": {
    "id": "51e5ab57-8960-42c2-8de1-99a41fae46b0",
    "type": "agent_run",
    "collection_id": "9bafeb94-3f6e-4d03-bd01-1ae8476e3f6e"
  }
}
```

### Saved output: d7a81f56-8aa0-47c2-aa87-bda452ff4c6a

#### Explanation

The failed run’s likely decisive error was its Nginx dev mapping: it combined an `alias` with `try_files $uri $uri/`, which can make `/dev/index.html` resolve incorrectly under the aliased directory and return 404 \[T0B5:&lt;RANGE&gt;location /dev/ {
        alias /var/www/project/dev/;
        try_files $uri $uri/ =404;
    }&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. The successful run used a straightforward alias plus `index index.html` instead \[T1B3:&lt;RANGE&gt;location /dev/ {
        alias /var/www/deploy/dev/;
        index index.html;
    }&lt;/RANGE&gt;\], and that configuration passed the hidden tests \[R1M.scores.reward\]. The failed hook also unnecessarily ran recursive `chown` as the unprivileged Git user \[T0B7:&lt;RANGE&gt;chown -R git:www-data "$target"&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] without adding that user to `www-data`, creating another possible hook error. Finally, it declared completion after only syntax-checking and reloading Nginx \[T0B8:&lt;RANGE&gt;nginx: configuration file /etc/nginx/nginx.conf test is successful&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] rather than performing a test push and requesting both HTTPS URLs, so the bad dev routing was never detected.

#### Cause Summary

Incorrect Nginx alias routing likely made the dev endpoint return 404.

### Saved output: d391b2b8-dff4-4a6e-8800-5afea81a112c

#### Explanation

The failed run misidentified the vulnerability as broad exception suppression in `MultiDict.get` and changed that API’s conversion semantics before establishing the actual failing behavior \[T0B30:&lt;RANGE&gt;except Exception:
            pass&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] \[T0B33:&lt;RANGE&gt;raise ValueError(
                'Failed to convert value for key %r using %r' % (key, type)&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. Its own test run then exposed the decisive issue—HTTP header control characters were accepted instead of raising `ValueError` \[T0B40:&lt;RANGE&gt;FAILED test/test_environ.py::TestResponse::test_prevent_control_characters_in_headers - AssertionError: ValueError not raised by append&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]—and it added suitable header validation, but still reported the incorrect CWE-703 \[T0B61:&lt;RANGE&gt;def _validate_header_value(value, what):&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] \[T0B67:&lt;RANGE&gt;{"file_path": "/app/bottle.py", "cwe_id": \["cwe-703"\]}&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. By contrast, the successful run used the baseline test failure to identify CRLF/header injection as CWE-93 \[T1B12:&lt;RANGE&gt;FAILED test/test_environ.py::TestResponse::test_prevent_control_characters_in_headers - AssertionError: ValueError not raised by append&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] and wrote the expected report entry \[T1B21:&lt;RANGE&gt;{"file_path": "/app/bottle.py", "cwe_id": \["cwe-93"\]}&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. Thus, despite passing visible tests after its header fix, the failed run likely failed hidden grading because the report contained the wrong exact CWE ID and because it retained an unrelated behavioral change to `MultiDict.get`.

#### Cause Summary

It reported CWE-703 instead of CWE-93 and made an unrelated API change.

### Saved output: 97dce3e2-b7ca-42f1-85a9-edde4508b0b2

#### Explanation

The failed run left the wrapper converting the large Python lists to NumPy arrays on every call \[T0B15:&lt;RANGE&gt;np_arr = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64)&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] and focused instead on repeated C-loop rewrites and OpenMP parallelization \[T0B77:&lt;RANGE&gt;Plan: 1) Overwrite portfolio_optimized.c with a parallelized version using OpenMP for the main loops, while releasing the GIL during heavy computation.&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. Although its final local benchmark passed, the 5000-asset result was only barely above the threshold—2058.8 ms versus 1699.6 ms and reported as 1.2x \[T0B82:&lt;RANGE&gt;Python baseline time: 2058.8 ms
Risk - Python: 0.022532625110, C: 0.022532625110, diff: 1.39e-17
Return - Python: 0.100448895074, C: 0.100448895074, diff: 8.33e-17
Correctness test PASSED
C optimized time: 1699.6 ms
Speedup: 1.2x
Performance requirement met! (1.2x &gt;= 1.2x)&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]—making it vulnerable to hidden-test or timing variation. The successful run recognized conversion overhead and cached converted arrays across repeated calls \[T1B17:&lt;RANGE&gt;Need to reduce overhead by caching converted numpy arrays in Python wrapper so repeated calls avoid expensive conversions&lt;/RANGE&gt;\], reducing the measured C path to 22.3 ms and providing a 63.5x margin at 5000 assets \[T1B18:&lt;RANGE&gt;C optimized time: 22.3 ms
Speedup: 63.5x
Performance requirement met! (63.5x &gt;= 1.2x)&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. Thus, the likely decisive failure was relying on a borderline, environment-sensitive speedup rather than eliminating the dominant repeated input-conversion cost.

#### Cause Summary

Repeated array conversion left performance too close to the required threshold.

```json
  }
}
```

### Saved output: adef1b96-708c-487d-b367-b8e4ef6837e7

#### Explanation

The most plausible failure was incomplete service activation: the failed run’s attempt to reload Nginx through systemd explicitly failed because systemd was unavailable \[T0B8:&lt;RANGE&gt;System has not been booted with systemd as init system (PID 1). Can't operate.
Failed to connect to bus: Host is down&lt;/RANGE&gt;\], after which it only issued a configuration reload \[T0B10:&lt;RANGE&gt;service nginx reload
 * Reloading nginx configuration nginx                                                                                                                   \[ OK \]&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. By contrast, the successful run explicitly created the SSH runtime directory, restarted SSH \[T1B4:&lt;RANGE&gt;mkdir -p /var/run/sshd
root@8a8ee52d86a4:/app# sed -i 's/^#\?PasswordAuthentication.*/PasswordAuthentication yes/' /etc/ssh/sshd_config&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] \[T1B4:&lt;RANGE&gt;service ssh restart
 * Restarting OpenBSD Secure Shell server sshd                                                                                                           \[ OK \]&lt;/RANGE&gt;\], and fully restarted Nginx after validating its configuration \[T1B4:&lt;RANGE&gt;nginx -t
nginx: the configuration file /etc/nginx/nginx.conf syntax is ok
nginx: configuration file /etc/nginx/nginx.conf test is successful
root@8a8ee52d86a4:/app# service nginx restart
 * Restarting nginx nginx                                                                                                                                \[ OK \]&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. The failed agent then declared completion without performing an actual SSH clone/push or HTTPS curl test \[T0B15:&lt;RANGE&gt;No further commands are required; the environment is ready for the described tests.&lt;/RANGE&gt;\], so it never caught whether the live services were using the intended configuration. Given that the repository and hook setup closely matched the passing approach, the missing clean service startup and end-to-end verification are the likeliest reasons the hidden tests failed.

#### Cause Summary

Services were not cleanly restarted or end-to-end tested before completion.

### Saved output: e05119d1-6581-4086-81c2-be05d383a42c

#### Explanation

The failed run most plausibly failed the image-comparison test because it mixed the GCC-specific configuration with the generic Unix implementation: it copied `GCCCONF.H` to `config.h` \[T0B37:&lt;RANGE&gt;cp MACHINE/UNIX/GCCCONF.H build/config.h&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] while still compiling `unix.c`, which caused an undefined `unix_init_povray` symbol \[T0B76:&lt;RANGE&gt;undefined reference to `unix_init_povray'&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. It patched only that symbol’s capitalization \[T0B83:&lt;RANGE&gt;text = text.replace('unix_init_povray()', 'unix_init_POVRAY()')&lt;/RANGE&gt;\], leaving a configuration/source pairing different from the known-good Unix build. By contrast, the successful run used `unixconf.h` directly \[T1B31:&lt;RANGE&gt;mv unixconf.h config.h&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] and then passed the render check \[T1B38:&lt;RANGE&gt;/app/deps/illum1.pov statistics&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. Although the failed run’s null-output sanity render also completed \[T0B90:&lt;RANGE&gt;/app/deps/illum1.pov statistics&lt;/RANGE&gt;\], that check did not compare rendered pixels, so it could not detect output differences caused by the mismatched build configuration.

#### Cause Summary

Used a mismatched GCC configuration and Unix implementation, likely altering rendered output.

### Saved output: 25041680-e09a-4bfe-9b56-8956833f7f58

#### Explanation

The failed run reconstructed the positional encoder with an extra embedding scale, setting `self.scale = math.sqrt(d_model)` and applying `x = x * self.scale` before adding positional values \[T0B13:&lt;RANGE&gt;self.scale = math.sqrt(d_model)&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] \[T0B13:&lt;RANGE&gt;x = x * self.scale
        x = x + self.pe\[:, :x.size(1)\]&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. That operation is not represented in the state dictionary, so loading all parameters successfully did not establish that the model’s forward behavior matched the original architecture. The successful run instead used the simpler positional operation `return x + self.pe\[:, :x.size(1)\]` \[T1B21:&lt;RANGE&gt;return x + self.pe\[:, :x.size(1)\]&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] and achieved a substantially lower tuned MSE of 0.01636 \[T1B22:&lt;RANGE&gt;Tuned MSE: 0.016358058899641037&lt;/RANGE&gt;\], compared with 0.07061 in the failed run \[T0B16:&lt;RANGE&gt;Tuned MSE: 0.0706128403544426&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. Although the failed run verified weight loading and that only output-layer parameters changed \[T0B16:&lt;RANGE&gt;Validated: TorchScript model can load the original weights.
Parameters changed: \['output_layer.weight', 'output_layer.bias'\]&lt;/RANGE&gt;\], it did not verify its forward semantics against the likely original positional-encoding behavior, making the extra scaling the plausible hidden-test failure.

#### Cause Summary

Incorrect extra embedding scaling changed the reconstructed model’s forward behavior.

### Saved output: dd323243-306b-496b-9c25-02c5479f02c3

#### Explanation

The failed run’s main weakness was premature and unreliable completion signaling: it attempted to mark the task complete before its final repository scan had returned, and that scan then failed because `secret\[` was treated as an invalid regular expression \[T0B17:&lt;RANGE&gt;git --no-pager grep -a 'secret\[' $(git rev-list --all)&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] \[T0B18:&lt;RANGE&gt;fatal: command line, 'secret\[': Invalid regular expression&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. Although it later corrected the grep with fixed-string matching, that check covered only the working tree and reachable commits \[T0B19:&lt;RANGE&gt;git --no-pager grep -F 'secret\[' $(git rev-list --all)&lt;/RANGE&gt;\], rather than explicitly confirming that no unreachable objects remained. The successful run performed the decisive post-cleanup check with `git fsck --no-reflogs --unreachable --no-progress`, which returned no objects, and then separately confirmed that reachable history contained no secret \[T1B15:&lt;RANGE&gt;git fsck --no-reflogs --unreachable --no-progress&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] \[T1B16:&lt;RANGE&gt;root@d9a257dabb03:/app/repo# git fsck --no-reflogs --unreachable --no-progress
root@d9a257dabb03:/app/repo# git --no-pager grep -n 'secret' $(git rev-list --all)&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. Thus, despite apparently performing the cleanup, the failed run did not finish with the same explicit, successful verification of all unreachable repository objects before completion.

#### Cause Summary

Premature completion and incomplete final verification of unreachable Git objects.

### Saved output: 67f4c8ec-2184-430b-ab4f-390b365cf378

#### Explanation

The failed run embedded whole-file `:%s` commands in macros a and b, then invoked each macro once per line with `:%normal!`, effectively repeating million-row substitutions up to a million times \[T0B16:&lt;RANGE&gt;call setreg('a', ":%s/^\\s*\\(\\S\\+\\)\\s*,\\s*\\(\\S\\+\\)\\s*,\\s*\\(\\S\\+\\)\\s*$/\\3;\\2;\\1;OK/\&lt;CR&gt;")
     2  call setreg('b', ":%s/.*/\\U&/\&lt;CR&gt;")&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] \[T0B16:&lt;RANGE&gt;:%normal! @a
     5  :%normal! @b&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. This caused Vim to run for so long that later verification commands were queued rather than executed, after which the agent spent many turns repeatedly sending newlines, checks, and interrupts instead of simply waiting \[T0B18:&lt;RANGE&gt;Previous response had warnings:
WARNINGS: - Command 1 should end with newline when followed by another command. Otherwise the two commands will be concatenated together on the same line.&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] \[T0B45:&lt;RANGE&gt;Send Ctrl+C to interrupt any running or pending command, wait briefly, then press Enter to confirm we have a clean prompt&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. Although it eventually switched to per-line macros, including `0gU$`, this happened only near the end and was not completed or verified before the timeout \[T0B83:&lt;RANGE&gt;call setreg('b', "0gU$")&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] \[R0M.exception\]. By contrast, the successful run used only per-line operations—space removal, `gUU`, and field reordering—then patiently polled until Vim finished and confirmed an exact match with `cmp` \[T1B5:&lt;RANGE&gt;call setreg('a', ":s/ //g\r")
call setreg('b', "gUU")
call setreg('c', ":s/\\(\[^,\]*\\),\\(\[^,\]*\\),\\(\[^,\]*\\)/\\3;\\2;\\1;OK\r")&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] \[T1B14:&lt;RANGE&gt;match&lt;/RANGE&gt;\].

#### Cause Summary

Whole-file substitutions were repeated per row, causing runaway runtime and timeout.

Otherwise the two commands will be concatenated together on the same line.&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. It then repeatedly resent installation, download, launch, and test batches while the terminal merely displayed queued command text, consuming the wall-clock limit without confirming execution \[T0B8:&lt;RANGE&gt;ls -a
ls model_cache
ls model_cache/sentiment_model
cat app.py
pip install --quiet --upgrade flask transformers torch&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] \[R0M.exception\]. The final recovery attempt made matters worse by leaving an unfinished pipeline, `pkill -f app.py |`, blocking further progress \[T0B14:&lt;RANGE&gt;pkill -f app.py |&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. By contrast, the successful run recovered its prompt, completed the model download \[T1B34:&lt;RANGE&gt;Model saved to /app/model_cache/sentiment_model&lt;/RANGE&gt;\], confirmed Flask was listening on 0.0.0.0:5000 \[T1B36:&lt;RANGE&gt; * Running on all addresses (0.0.0.0)&lt;/RANGE&gt;\], and verified a valid 200 response with sentiment and confidence scores \[T1B36:&lt;RANGE&gt;200
{"confidence":{"negative":0.00012237548071425408,"positive":0.9998775720596313},"sentiment":"positive"}&lt;/RANGE&gt;\].

### Saved output: 52d20b44-227b-4fd5-836f-552132f8452d

#### Explanation

The failed run likely broke the grader’s expected source layout by extracting with `--strip-components=1`, placing the source files directly in `/app/sqlite` \[T0B1:&lt;RANGE&gt;tar -xzf /app/vendor/sqlite-fossil-release.tar.gz -C /app/sqlite --strip-components=1&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. By contrast, the successful run preserved the archive’s top-level directory, yielding `/app/sqlite/sqlite`, and consistently configured and built from that directory \[T1B2:&lt;RANGE&gt;root@baa1672ea998:/app# ls /app/sqlite
sqlite&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] \[T1B3:&lt;RANGE&gt;cd /app/sqlite/sqlite && CFLAGS="--coverage" LDFLAGS="--coverage" ./configure --prefix=/app/sqlite/install&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. The failed run did successfully compile with coverage flags, install the binary, and verify `/usr/local/bin/sqlite3` \[T0B8:&lt;RANGE&gt;root@d7ccef77cc10:/app/sqlite# which sqlite3
/usr/local/bin/sqlite3&lt;/RANGE&gt;\], so missing compilation or PATH setup does not plausibly explain the zero reward. The decisive difference is therefore the altered on-disk source/build layout, which likely violated a hidden path expectation even though the CLI itself ran.

#### Cause Summary

Stripping the archive’s top directory likely violated the grader’s expected SQLite layout.

### Saved output: 403acd40-933f-415d-826d-69e26e4216d6

#### Explanation

The failed run used an incomplete locale warm-up: it constructed only the default locale and touched `ctype` and `num_put` before doing one `ostringstream` conversion \[T0B26:&lt;RANGE&gt;std::locale loc;
        (void)std::use_facet&lt;std::ctype&lt;char&gt;&gt;(loc);
        (void)std::use_facet&lt;std::num_put&lt;char&gt;&gt;(loc);&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. Although this happened to stop the observed executable from crashing and produced zero Valgrind errors \[T0B28:&lt;RANGE&gt;ERROR SUMMARY: 0 errors from 0 contexts (suppressed: 0 from 0)&lt;/RANGE&gt;\], it did not robustly initialize all locale facets and paths used by the modified libstdc++. The successful run instead constructed the environment locale, explicitly initialized `ctype`, `num_put`, `num_get`, and `numpunct` \[T1B18:&lt;RANGE&gt;std::locale loc("");
        (void)std::use_facet&lt;std::ctype&lt;char&gt;&gt;(loc);
        (void)std::use_facet&lt;std::num_put&lt;char&gt;&gt;(loc);
        (void)std::use_facet&lt;std::num_get&lt;char&gt;&gt;(loc);
        (void)std::use_facet&lt;std::numpunct&lt;char&gt;&gt;(loc);&lt;/RANGE&gt;\], and exercised `std::cout` locale installation before the custom heap was enabled \[T1B18:&lt;RANGE&gt;auto previous_loc = std::cout.getloc();
        std::cout.imbue(loc);
        std::cout.flush();
        std::cout.imbue(previous_loc);&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. Thus, the failed fix likely passed its narrow manual check but missed the fuller initialization required by hidden tests.

#### Cause Summary

The locale warm-up was too narrow to satisfy hidden runtime paths.

### Saved output: b0d53f69-1a21-4049-9a65-0fc53062c369

#### Explanation

The failed run used Hugging Face’s `cache_dir` mechanism rather than saving a standalone model directly at the required path \[T0B3:&lt;RANGE&gt;AutoModelForSequenceClassification.from_pretrained(MODEL_NAME, cache_dir=CACHE_DIR)&lt;/RANGE&gt;\], so the files were likely nested in the Hub cache structure instead of being loadable from `/app/model_cache/sentiment_model` itself. By contrast, the successful run explicitly called `tokenizer.save_pretrained(save_dir)` and `model.save_pretrained(save_dir)` \[T1B34:&lt;RANGE&gt;tokenizer.save_pretrained(save_dir)
model.save_pretrained(save_dir)
print("Model saved to", save_dir)&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] and loaded the model directly from that directory \[T1B34:&lt;RANGE&gt;tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(MODEL_DIR)
model = AutoModelForSequenceClassification.from_pretrained(MODEL_DIR)&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. The failed API also only checked whether `text` existed \[T0B3:&lt;RANGE&gt;if not data or 'text' not in data:
        return jsonify({"error": "Invalid request. Provide 'text' field."}), 400&lt;/RANGE&gt;\], whereas the successful implementation returned HTTP 400 for a non-string value as required by the request schema \[T1B34:&lt;RANGE&gt;if not isinstance(text, str):
        return jsonify({"error": "'text' must be a string."}), 400&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. Although the failed run verified one valid positive request successfully \[T0B8:&lt;RANGE&gt;{"confidence":{"negative":0.00011442825780250132,"positive":0.9998855590820312},"sentiment":"positive"}&lt;/RANGE&gt;\], that did not verify the required on-disk layout or malformed-input behavior likely exercised by hidden tests.

#### Cause Summary

Model was cached indirectly and input validation was incomplete.

### Saved output: 6c38d7f0-c88c-49a8-b5b4-044fffd391f1

#### Explanation

The failed run timed out before performing the required file moves or creating the CSV; it remained in a repetitive inspection loop, repeatedly proposing to preview the same extracted text files \[T0B45:&lt;RANGE&gt;Plan: List text files and view each one’s contents (first ~40 lines) for analysis.&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] \[T0B49:&lt;RANGE&gt;Plan: Sequentially preview each text file using `head -n 40` to review contents for classification and data extraction.&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. Although it had already extracted OCR/PDF text and identified clear invoice values such as Total $6558 versus Amount due $4382 \[T0B60:&lt;RANGE&gt;SubTotal: $6558
Total: $6558

Amount due: $4382 USD&lt;/RANGE&gt;\], it never consolidated the classifications and acted on them. By contrast, the successful run created both destination directories, moved every file, and wrote the required summary in one script \[T1B55:&lt;RANGE&gt;invoice_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
other_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
for item in docs.iterdir():
    target = invoice_dir if item.name in invoice_lookup else other_dir
    shutil.move(str(item), target / item.name)&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. It then verified that the source directory was empty and displayed the completed CSV with its aggregate row \[T1B58:&lt;RANGE&gt;root@29700baac5ad:/app# ls documents
root@29700baac5ad:/app# ls invoices&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] \[T1B58:&lt;RANGE&gt;filename,total_amount,vat_amount
2lgKzDuI4E4g.jpg,6558.00,0.00&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] \[T1B58:&lt;RANGE&gt;total,81315.20,5402.48&lt;/RANGE&gt;\].

#### Cause Summary

Timed out repeatedly inspecting text without moving files or creating the CSV.

### Saved output: 4ed88a8d-2c53-442c-a486-a6850d994827

#### Explanation

The failed run incorrectly treated the example address 4194304 as a required load base, despite confirming that the PIE’s actual PT_LOAD virtual addresses began at zero \[T0B10:&lt;RANGE&gt;LOAD           0x0000000000000000 0x0000000000000000 0x0000000000000000&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. It then hard-coded a 0x400000 adjustment for ET_DYN files \[T0B25:&lt;RANGE&gt;const DEFAULT_BASE = 0x400000;&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] and reported success merely because key 4194304 appeared \[T0B26:&lt;RANGE&gt;has4194304 true&lt;/RANGE&gt;\], causing every output address to differ from the reference. By contrast, the successful run retained raw `p_vaddr` values \[T1B5:&lt;RANGE&gt;const addr = p_vaddr + offset;&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] and verified output beginning at address zero \[T1B12:&lt;RANGE&gt;{"0":1179403647,"4":65794,"8":0&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. The failed version also emitted zero-padded words through `p_memsz`, including partial words and BSS \[T0B25:&lt;RANGE&gt;const groups = Math.ceil(p_memsz / 4);&lt;/RANGE&gt;\], whereas the passing version conservatively emitted only complete four-byte words from `p_filesz` \[T1B5:&lt;RANGE&gt;for (let offset = 0; offset + 4 &lt;= segment.length; offset += 4)&lt;/RANGE&gt;\].

#### Cause Summary

Hard-coded PIE rebasing produced incorrect addresses and extra synthetic memory values.

### Saved output: ec874844-a34c-4ed8-913f-c61cbee75c85

#### Explanation

The failed run timed out because it spent most of its wall-clock budget repeatedly tweaking unreadable ASCII/OCR renderings and installing large tools, including Tesseract and later Chafa \[T0B19:&lt;RANGE&gt;Plan: Compute automatic threshold (Otsu)&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] \[T0B131:&lt;RANGE&gt;apt-get install -y chafa&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. Crucially, it already had OCR output sufficient to reconstruct the intended pseudocode—SHA-256 of the image, followed by SHA-256 of that digest, its first 10 bytes, and a salt—but continued investigating OCR ambiguities instead of testing the natural interpretation against the provided prefix \[T0B168:&lt;RANGE&gt;hO = cha256(img_ bytes)
# cha2z56(ho // hof:10\] \[/ SALT)
H = cha256(ho, hof:10\], SALT)&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. The successful run made the reasonable corrections, computed the result using `salt = b"0000TBENCH-SALT"` and `h0 + h0\[:10\] + salt`, and wrote it to `output.txt` \[T1B57:&lt;RANGE&gt;salt = b"0000TBENCH-SALT"
data = pathlib.Path('code.png').read_bytes()
h0 = hashlib.sha256(data).digest()
h = hashlib.sha256(h0 + h0\[:10\] + salt).hexdigest()

### Saved output: 9bc31558-b8a9-4233-91a9-23559d49eac6

#### Explanation

The failed run changed into `/app/warriors` \[T0B3:&lt;RANGE&gt;cd warriors
&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] and then created `my_warrior.red` in that directory \[T0B43:&lt;RANGE&gt;cat &lt;&lt;'EOF' &gt; my_warrior.red&lt;/RANGE&gt;\], rather than at the required `/app/my_warrior.red` path. Consequently, its apparently passing results—76 wins against Stone, 81 against Paper, 83 against Vampire, 33 against Snake, and 43 against G2-Clear \[T0B44:&lt;RANGE&gt;Results: 76 0 24&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] \[T0B44:&lt;RANGE&gt;Results: 81 0 19&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] \[T0B44:&lt;RANGE&gt;Results: 83 6 11&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] \[T0B44:&lt;RANGE&gt;Results: 33 56 11&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] \[T0B44:&lt;RANGE&gt;Results: 43 55 2&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]—validated the wrong file location. The successful run remained in `/app`, wrote `my_warrior.red` there, and tested it using opponent paths under `warriors/` \[T1B91:&lt;RANGE&gt;cat &lt;&lt;'EOF' &gt; my_warrior.red&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] \[T1B91:&lt;RANGE&gt;pmars -b -r 100 -f my_warrior.red warriors/vampire.red&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. Thus, the failed run solved the matchup tuning but submitted no warrior at the path the hidden grader expected.

#### Cause Summary

The warrior was written and tested in the wrong directory.

### Saved output: b88d9339-c57e-48fc-9924-a3c026bf8aa6

#### Explanation

The failed run introduced an ABI-risky source modification: its Python edit removed the legacy `malloc`/`free` declarations, but the conditional intended to add standard headers did not execute because it tested for a comment delimiter already present in the file \[T0B63:&lt;RANGE&gt;if insert.split('\n')\[0\] not in text:&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] \[T0B63:&lt;RANGE&gt;text = text.replace(old_block, "/* Standard library prototypes are provided by the includes above. */", 1)&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. The resulting rebuild explicitly warned that `malloc`, `free`, and `exit` were implicitly declared \[T0B64:&lt;RANGE&gt;warning: incompatible implicit declaration of built-in function ‘malloc’&lt;/RANGE&gt;\], which is unsafe on a 64-bit system even though the one sanity render happened to complete. It also departed from the supplied build settings by changing optimization and compatibility flags to `-O2 -std=gnu89 -fcommon` \[T0B47:&lt;RANGE&gt;sed -i 's/^CFLAGS=.*/CFLAGS= -c -O2 -std=gnu89 -fcommon/' Makefile&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. By contrast, the successful run compiled with the supplied `-O` configuration \[T1B36:&lt;RANGE&gt;cc -c -O povray.c&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] and its installed binary passed the same render with the expected statistics \[T1B38:&lt;RANGE&gt;/app/deps/illum1.pov statistics&lt;/RANGE&gt;\], avoiding the failed run’s unnecessary and defective header changes.

#### Cause Summary

A defective header edit left unsafe implicit allocation declarations in the build.

#### Explanation

The failed run recovered the correct secret and wrote it to the correct path \[T0B10:&lt;RANGE&gt;printf 'secret\[lost_and_found_in_git\]\n' &gt; /app/secret.txt&lt;/RANGE&gt;\], but it relied on ordinary `git gc --prune=now` rather than forcing a full repack \[T0B10:&lt;RANGE&gt;git gc --prune=now&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. Its verification also searched only reachable log patches and truncated `git fsck` through `head` \[T0B11:&lt;RANGE&gt;git fsck --no-reflogs | head&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] \[T0B11:&lt;RANGE&gt;git log -p --all | grep -n "secret\\["&lt;/RANGE&gt;\], which may miss residual secret bytes in repository storage. By contrast, the successful run explicitly enumerated the unreachable secret-bearing commit and blob \[T1B12:&lt;RANGE&gt;unreachable commit 6b4c75caa9a2aa9a630e16764007b0109b1f5c07
unreachable blob 52d18a091cd9755bacb8eaccab2882a24b080dc7&lt;/RANGE&gt;\], then used aggressive garbage collection and an untruncated unreachable-object check \[T1B15:&lt;RANGE&gt;git gc --prune=now --aggressive&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] \[T1B16:&lt;RANGE&gt;git fsck --no-reflogs --unreachable --no-progress&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. The most plausible cause is therefore incomplete physical cleanup or insufficient verification in the failed run, leaving something detectable by the hidden repository scan despite normal Git commands no longer exposing it.

#### Cause Summary

Non-aggressive cleanup and narrow verification likely left hidden residual secret data.

### Saved output: 434f26f9-c965-4281-a703-4faa87d0af78

#### Explanation

The failed run left substantial Python-wrapper overhead because every call converted the large nested covariance matrix into a contiguous NumPy array, so even after the symmetry-aware C optimization its 5000-asset runtime was 1330.4 ms versus a 1601.3 ms baseline—only barely meeting the threshold in that particular run \[T0B32:&lt;RANGE&gt;C optimized time: 1330.4 ms
Speedup: 1.2x
Performance requirement met! (1.2x &gt;= 1.2x)&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. This margin was fragile: its earlier implementation had already measured below the requirement at 5000 assets \[T0B30:&lt;RANGE&gt;C optimized time: 1304.3 ms
Speedup: 1.2x
Performance requirement NOT met (1.2x &lt; 1.2x)&lt;/RANGE&gt;\], making a hidden performance failure plausible despite the visible benchmark eventually passing. The successful run identified conversion overhead as the issue and cached converted arrays for repeated calls \[T1B17:&lt;RANGE&gt;Need to reduce overhead by caching converted numpy arrays in Python wrapper so repeated calls avoid expensive conversions&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. That produced a robust 63.5x speedup at 5000 assets \[T1B18:&lt;RANGE&gt;C optimized time: 22.3 ms
Speedup: 63.5x
Performance requirement met! (63.5x &gt;= 1.2x)&lt;/RANGE&gt;\], unlike the failed run’s borderline result.

#### Cause Summary

Repeated NumPy conversions left performance too close to the required threshold.

```json
  }
```

### Saved output: 5846bebf-aaff-4b18-a279-e8faba1239fa

#### Explanation

The failed run reconstructed the forward pass incorrectly by multiplying both source and target embeddings by √128 before positional encoding \[T0B27:&lt;RANGE&gt;src_emb = self.embedding(src) * self._scale&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] \[T0B27:&lt;RANGE&gt;tgt_emb = self.embedding(tgt) * self._scale&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. The successful run instead fed the linear embeddings directly into positional encoding, matching the expected architecture \[T1B21:&lt;RANGE&gt;src_emb = self.pos_encoder(self.embedding(src))
        tgt_emb = self.pos_encoder(self.embedding(tgt))&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. Because this scaling is not represented in the state dictionary, strict weight loading still succeeded, and the failed run’s self-check only established improvement under its own incorrect forward function, from 1.55626559 to 0.07071855 \[T0B34:&lt;RANGE&gt;Original MSE: 1.55626559
Updated MSE: 0.07071855&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. By contrast, the correct forward pass plus least-squares output-layer fit reached 0.016358, consistent with the hidden grader’s expected model behavior \[T1B22:&lt;RANGE&gt;Baseline MSE: 1.5510306358337402
Tuned MSE: 0.016358058899641037&lt;/RANGE&gt;\].

#### Cause Summary

Incorrect √d_model embedding scaling changed the reconstructed model’s forward architecture.

### Saved output: d7e272a3-68a3-493c-9b41-d9f1482d000c

#### Explanation

The failed run spent essentially its entire wall-clock budget installing R and repeatedly polling the long-running package installation \[T0B5:&lt;RANGE&gt;apt-get install -y r-base
&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] \[T0B7:&lt;RANGE&gt;Wait for the installation to finish before proceeding with further steps.&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. Although installation finally returned to the shell prompt \[T0B70:&lt;RANGE&gt;root@00986b04263c:/app#&lt;/RANGE&gt;\], the agent timed out before creating `/app/ars.R`, implementing `ars` or `test`, generating a sample file, or running any verification. By contrast, the successful run proceeded after confirming R at `/usr/bin/R` \[T1B70:&lt;RANGE&gt;/usr/bin/R&lt;/RANGE&gt;\], wrote a 271-line `/app/ars.R` \[T1B82:&lt;RANGE&gt;271 /app/ars.R&lt;/RANGE&gt;\], and executed its test function, producing a passing normal-distribution result \[T1B84:&lt;RANGE&gt;NORMAL_TEST: PASS (mean=0.007, sd=0.994)&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. Thus, the decisive failure was time management around dependency installation, leaving none of the required deliverables present for hidden grading.

#### Cause Summary

R installation consumed the timeout before any required implementation was created.

### Saved output: 80ce1ec3-885d-4ac6-b011-4407f2962e8d

#### Explanation

The failed run produced posterior means of 2.890077 and 16.45294 \[T0B56:&lt;RANGE&gt;2.890077&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] \[T0B56:&lt;RANGE&gt;16.45294&lt;/RANGE&gt;\], whereas the successful run obtained 2.8579150123 and 16.2674627884 \[T1B40:&lt;RANGE&gt;2.8579150123&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] \[T1B40:&lt;RANGE&gt;16.2674627884&lt;/RANGE&gt;\], so the failed estimates likely fell outside the hidden tests’ numerical tolerance. Its sampling also reported 177 post-warmup divergent transitions \[T0B54:&lt;RANGE&gt;There were 177 divergent transitions after warmup.&lt;/RANGE&gt;\], yet the agent merely verified that files existed and marked the task complete \[T0B55:&lt;RANGE&gt;Need to verify the output files (posterior means and scripts) and their contents.&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. By comparison, the passing run had only 77 divergences \[T1B40:&lt;RANGE&gt;There were 77 divergent transitions after warmup.&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] and computed means directly from extracted posterior draws \[T1B5:&lt;RANGE&gt;post &lt;- rstan::extract(fit, pars = c("alpha", "beta"))
alpha_mean &lt;- mean(post$alpha)
beta_mean &lt;- mean(post$beta)&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. The decisive failure was therefore inaccurate, insufficiently diagnosed MCMC output rather than missing files or an RStan installation failure.

#### Cause Summary

Posterior estimates missed tolerance after a highly divergent, insufficiently validated MCMC run.

### Saved output: 7664bdd2-387e-47cf-a6e5-85d19f2ab49d

#### Explanation

The failed run fixed only the free-run branch by advancing `p` by the encoded run length and continuing \[T0B73:&lt;RANGE&gt;p += wh * (1 + Wosize_hd(hd));\n        continue;&lt;/RANGE&gt;\], but the bootstrap still segfaulted afterward \[T0B78:&lt;RANGE&gt;make\[2\]: *** \[Makefile:147: camlinternalFormatBasics.cmi\] Segmentation fault (core dumped)&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. It missed the decisive bug identified in the successful run: live pool slots must advance by the fixed size-class width `wh`, not the object's `Whsize_hd(hd)` \[T1B153:&lt;RANGE&gt;old = "      p += Whsize_hd(hd);\n"&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] \[T1B155:&lt;RANGE&gt;"      p += wh;\n"&lt;/RANGE&gt;\]. After that correction, the successful run's build progressed far beyond the original coldstart failure into native compiler compilation \[T1B170:&lt;RANGE&gt;OCAMLOPT typing/signature_group.cmx&lt;/RANGE&gt;\], whereas the failed run consumed its remaining time on repeated source inspection and GDB sessions \[T0B79\] \[T0B95\]. It ultimately timed out without completing a successful rebuild or running the required basic tests, and even briefly made an unsupported completion claim before acknowledging that the build still crashed \[T0B91:&lt;RANGE&gt;The OCaml runtime now builds successfully (make -j4 world.opt) and the basic testsuite (make -C testsuite one DIR=tests/basic) passes.&lt;/RANGE&gt;\] \[T0B93:&lt;RANGE&gt;The previous build still segfaults during the coldstart stage&lt;/RANGE&gt;\].

#### Cause Summary

It fixed the wrong sweep increment and timed out before verification.
